Historically described as a highly alcoholic spirit, it is 45–74% ABV (alcohol by volume). Absinthe traditionally has a natural green color but may also be colorless. It is commonly referred to in historical literature as la fée verte 'the green fairy'. While sometimes casually referred to as a liqueur, absinthe unlike pastis is not usually bottled with sugar or sweeteners. Absinthe is traditionally bottled at a high level of alcohol by volume, but it is normally diluted with water before being consumed.","default":{"$schema":"../../schemas/category.schema.json","name":"Absinthe","categoryType":"spirit","description":"Absinthe is an anise-flavored spirit derived from several plants, including the flowers and leaves of grand wormwood, together with green anise, sweet fennel, and other medicinal and culinary herbs. Historically described as a highly alcoholic spirit, it is 45–74% ABV (alcohol by volume). Absinthe traditionally has a natural green color but may also be colorless. It is commonly referred to in historical literature as la fée verte 'the green fairy'. While sometimes casually referred to as a liqueur, absinthe unlike pastis is not usually bottled with sugar or sweeteners. Absinthe is traditionally bottled at a high level of alcohol by volume, but it is normally diluted with water before being consumed.","default":"$0:data:0:rsc:props:children:0:props:children:props:relatedRecipes:2:ingredients:0:default","refs":"$0:data:0:rsc:props:children:0:props:children:props:relatedRecipes:2:ingredients:0:refs","parents":"$0:data:0:rsc:props:children:0:props:children:props:relatedRecipes:2:ingredients:0:parents","slug":"absinthe","type":"category","quantity":{"amount":0.25,"unit":"tsp"}},{"$schema":"../../schemas/category.schema.json","name":"Falernum","categoryType":"liqueur","description":"Falernum is a Barbados-born flavoring of lime, almond, ginger, and clove. Historically described as a highly alcoholic spirit, it is 45–74% ABV (alcohol by volume). Absinthe traditionally has a natural green color but may also be colorless. It is commonly referred to in historical literature as la fée verte 'the green fairy'. While sometimes casually referred to as a liqueur, absinthe unlike pastis is not usually bottled with sugar or sweeteners.
